			window.dataLayer = window.dataLayer || [];
      function gtag() { window.dataLayer.push(arguments); }
          
      var ONETRUST_CATEGORIES = {
        strictlyNecessary: 'C0001',
        performance: 'C0002',
        functional: 'C0003',
        targeting: 'C0004',
        socialMedia: 'C0005',
      };
    
      function getConsentState(category, groups) {
        return groups.includes(category) ? 'granted' : 'denied';
      }
    
      window.addEventListener('OneTrustGroupsUpdated', event => {
        window.gtag('consent', 'default', {
          ad_storage: getConsentState(ONETRUST_CATEGORIES.targeting, event.detail),
          ad_user_data: getConsentState(ONETRUST_CATEGORIES.targeting, event.detail),
          ad_personalization: getConsentState(ONETRUST_CATEGORIES.targeting, event.detail),

          personalization_storage: getConsentState(ONETRUST_CATEGORIES.strictlyNecessary, event.detail),
          security_storage: getConsentState(ONETRUST_CATEGORIES.strictlyNecessary, event.detail),
          'wait_for_update': 500,
        });
      });
    
      function OptanonWrapper() {
        if (OnetrustActiveGroups.indexOf('C0002') != -1 && OnetrustActiveGroups.indexOf('C0004') != -1) { 
          gtag('consent', 'update', { 
            'analytics_storage': 'granted',
            'ad_storage': 'granted'
          });
        } else if (OnetrustActiveGroups.indexOf('C0002') != -1) {
          gtag('consent', 'update', { 'analytics_storage': 'granted' });
        } else if (OnetrustActiveGroups.indexOf('C0004') != -1) { 
          gtag('consent', 'update', { 'ad_storage': 'granted' }); 
        };
      }

<main  id="content" class="site-main post-1444 post type-post status-publish format-standard has-post-thumbnail hentry category-basics">

			<div  class="page-header">
			<h1 class="entry-title">What Is Blockchain And How Does It Work? Crypto Guide</h1>		</div>
	
	<div  class="page-content">
				<div  data-elementor-type="wp-post" data-elementor-id="1444" class="elementor elementor-1444" data-elementor-post-type="post">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-ace2537 e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="ace2537" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-6a278a2 elementor-widget elementor-widget-text-editor" data-id="6a278a2" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="text-editor.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
									<p>📕 <strong>Definition</strong>: <em>A blockchain represents a digital ledger this is public, decentralized, and very difficult to tamper with. </em></p>								</div>
				</div>
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-3c24966 elementor-widget elementor-widget-global elementor-global-1592 elementor-widget-text-editor" data-id="3c24966" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="text-editor.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
									<p>Written by: <a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/mike">Mike Martin</a>&nbsp; &nbsp;|&nbsp; <em>Updated September 5, 2024</em></p>
<p>Reviewed by: <a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/ryan-grace">Ryan Grace</a></p>
<p>Fact checked by: <a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/laurence-willows">Laurence Willows</a></p>								</div>
				</div>
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-4af461a elementor-widget elementor-widget-image" data-id="4af461a" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="image.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
															<img fetchpriority="high" decoding="async" width="768" height="404" src="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/blockchain-768x404.png" class="attachment-medium_large size-medium_large wp-image-1607" alt="blockchain grid" srcset="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/blockchain-768x404.png 768w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/blockchain-300x158.png 300w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/blockchain-1024x539.png 1024w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/blockchain.png 1520w" sizes="(max-width: 768px) 100vw, 768px" />															</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-5debfda e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="5debfda" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-9003ad7 elementor-widget elementor-widget-text-editor" data-id="9003ad7" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="text-editor.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
									<p class="preFade fadeIn">Without blockchain technology, popular cryptocurrencies like bitcoin, ether and litecoin would not exist. It is therefore vital to have a core understanding of this foundational technology before mastering cryptocurrency. </p><p class="preFade fadeIn">In this article, we will teach you what blockchain is, show you how it works, and explain how cryptocurrencies come into existence.</p>								</div>
				</div>
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-8d1c9b6 elementor-toc--minimized-on-mobile elementor-widget elementor-widget-table-of-contents" data-id="8d1c9b6" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;headings_by_tags&quot;:[&quot;h2&quot;],&quot;exclude_headings_by_selector&quot;:[],&quot;marker_view&quot;:&quot;bullets&quot;,&quot;minimized_on&quot;:&quot;mobile&quot;,&quot;icon&quot;:{&quot;value&quot;:&quot;fas fa-circle&quot;,&quot;library&quot;:&quot;fa-solid&quot;,&quot;rendered_tag&quot;:&quot;&lt;svg class=\&quot;e-font-icon-svg e-fas-circle\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 512 512\&quot; xmlns=\&quot;http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\&quot;&gt;&lt;path d=\&quot;M256 8C119 8 8 119 8 256s111 248 248 248 248-111 248-248S393 8 256 8z\&quot;&gt;&lt;\/path&gt;&lt;\/svg&gt;&quot;},&quot;no_headings_message&quot;:&quot;No headings were found on this page.&quot;,&quot;minimize_box&quot;:&quot;yes&quot;,&quot;hierarchical_view&quot;:&quot;yes&quot;,&quot;min_height&quot;:{&quot;unit&quot;:&quot;px&quot;,&quot;size&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;sizes&quot;:[]},&quot;min_height_tablet&quot;:{&quot;unit&quot;:&quot;px&quot;,&quot;size&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;sizes&quot;:[]},&quot;min_height_mobile&quot;:{&quot;unit&quot;:&quot;px&quot;,&quot;size&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;sizes&quot;:[]},&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="table-of-contents.default">

				Table of Contents			</div>

		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-06d1a4e e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="06d1a4e" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-c7b287e elementor-widget-divider--view-line elementor-widget elementor-widget-global elementor-global-1612 elementor-widget-divider" data-id="c7b287e" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="divider.default">

			<span class="elementor-divider-separator">

		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-bd3005d e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="bd3005d" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-bd5ee6c elementor-widget elementor-widget-global elementor-global-1610 elementor-widget-text-editor" data-id="bd5ee6c" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="text-editor.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
									<p>🍒 tasty takeaways</p>								</div>
				</div>
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-77d3a4e elementor-widget elementor-widget-text-editor" data-id="77d3a4e" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="text-editor.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
									<ul><li>Blockchain is a decentralized ledger for transactions.</li><li>It ensures security, transparency, and immutability.</li><li>Public blockchains are open to all, while private ones are controlled.</li><li>Consensus mechanisms validate transactions in cryptocurrencies.</li><li>NFTs represent unique digital assets on blockchains.</li></ul>								</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-1720f7f e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="1720f7f" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-6fcac0c elementor-widget elementor-widget-heading" data-id="6fcac0c" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="heading.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
					<h2 class="elementor-heading-title elementor-size-default">Blockchain Explained Simply</h2>				</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-44722b5 e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="44722b5" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-50d03b8 elementor-widget elementor-widget-image" data-id="50d03b8" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="image.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
															<img decoding="async" width="800" height="419" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20800%20419'%3E%3C/svg%3E" class="attachment-large size-large wp-image-1614" alt="what is blockchain?" data-lazy-srcset="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/what-is-blockchain-1024x536.png 1024w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/what-is-blockchain-300x157.png 300w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/what-is-blockchain-768x402.png 768w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/what-is-blockchain.png 1200w" data-lazy-sizes="(max-width: 800px) 100vw, 800px" data-lazy-src="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/what-is-blockchain-1024x536.png" /><noscript><img decoding="async" width="800" height="419" src="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/what-is-blockchain-1024x536.png" class="attachment-large size-large wp-image-1614" alt="what is blockchain?" srcset="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/what-is-blockchain-1024x536.png 1024w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/what-is-blockchain-300x157.png 300w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/what-is-blockchain-768x402.png 768w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/what-is-blockchain.png 1200w" sizes="(max-width: 800px) 100vw, 800px" /></noscript>															</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-0e67dc7 e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="0e67dc7" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-e58550a elementor-widget elementor-widget-text-editor" data-id="e58550a" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="text-editor.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
									<p class="preFade fadeIn">At its core, a blockchain is a ledger. A ledger simply represents a collection of transactions that have been verified and recorded. That’s all a blockchain is &#8211; a digital ledger that is distributed across all participants in a network. </p><p class="preFade fadeIn">A traditional (non-blockchain) ledger has many vulnerabilities. Usually, only a handful of people have access to these ledgers. This centralization of power can lead to ledgers being damaged, lost, and manipulated. </p><p class="preFade fadeIn">Distributed ledger technology, aka ‘blockchain’, solves all of the problems of traditional ledgers in 5 big ways: </p><ul><li class="preFade fadeIn"><h3>Blockchain Is Immutable</h3></li></ul><p class="preFade fadeIn">Unlike a traditional ledger, blockchain ledgers are immutable. Immutable simple means set in stone. Once a transaction gets put in a block, and that new block is added to the blockchain, it is impossible to change the details of that transaction. </p><p class="preFade fadeIn">In blockchain technology, ‘cooking the book’ is impossible. Even if you tried to, a blockchain community would see your attempt and stop it. This leads us to our next distinguishing feature of blockchain technology. </p><ul><li class="preFade fadeIn"><h3>Blockchain Is Public</h3></li></ul><p class="preFade fadeIn">Although both private and public blockchains exist, all of the blockchain networks you have heard of (e.g., Bitcoin and Ethereum) are public blockchains. Every single transaction that has ever occurred on a public blockchain is made available to the public to view.  This means that anyone in the world can audit the transactional history of any public blockchain. </p><p class="preFade fadeIn">Block explorers like <a href="https://www.blockchain.com/explorer">Blockchain.com</a> and <a href="https://etherscan.io/">Etherscan</a> allow anyone in the world to look up blockchain addresses and see every single transaction that that address was involved with.</p><ul><li class="preFade fadeIn"><h3>Blockchain is Decentralized</h3></li></ul><p class="preFade fadeIn">In centralized systems, a small group of people supervise and make decisions about the current and future state of an organization.</p><p class="preFade fadeIn">In decentralized blockchains like Bitcoin and Ethereum, that control is delegated to all participants within the network. In public blockchains, all users, collectively, have a say on the state of the network. </p><p class="preFade fadeIn">This means that blockchain participants do not have to trust one another in order for a network to be a success &#8211; every participant has a copy of the distributed ledger, and every participant has a say on the state of the network. This leads us to our next characteristic of blockchain: peer-to-peer.</p><ul><li class="preFade fadeIn"><h3>Blockchain is Peer-to-Peer</h3></li></ul><p class="preFade fadeIn">In public blockchains, peer-to-peer refers to a network of interconnected computers (nodes) that all have access to the same distributed ledger devoid of a central authority.  </p><p class="preFade fadeIn">This democratization of power helps to create a trustless system.</p><p class="preFade fadeIn">This concept of peer-to-peer is so integral to both blockchain and cryptocurrency that Satoshi Nakamoto, the inventor of Bitcoin, named his white paper, “<a href="https://bitcoin.org/bitcoin.pdf">Bitcoin: A Peer-to-Peer Electronic Cash System</a>.” </p><ul><li class="preFade fadeIn"><h3>Blockchain is Secure</h3></li></ul><p class="preFade fadeIn">When compared to traditional ledgers, blockchain introduces unparalleled security. We already mentioned that blockchains are immutable &#8211; once a transaction enters a block, and that block gets added to the blockchain, it can not be changed. </p><p class="preFade fadeIn">On top of this immutability, blockchain includes a ‘consensus mechanism’. Consensus simply means that a majority of the peers on a blockchain network must reach an agreement on the state of the network before a new block is added. They accomplish this by determining which transactions are valid and invalid within a block. </p><p class="preFade fadeIn">In the following sections, we will explore consensus in more detail.</p><h2 class="preFade fadeIn">How Blockchain Works in 7 Steps</h2>								</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-6377614 e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="6377614" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-ed7d577 elementor-widget elementor-widget-image" data-id="ed7d577" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="image.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
															<img decoding="async" width="768" height="576" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20768%20576'%3E%3C/svg%3E" class="attachment-large size-large wp-image-1615" alt="how blockchain works" data-lazy-srcset="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-blockchain-works.png 768w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-blockchain-works-300x225.png 300w" data-lazy-sizes="(max-width: 768px) 100vw, 768px" data-lazy-src="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-blockchain-works.png" /><noscript><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="768" height="576" src="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-blockchain-works.png" class="attachment-large size-large wp-image-1615" alt="how blockchain works" srcset="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-blockchain-works.png 768w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-blockchain-works-300x225.png 300w" sizes="(max-width: 768px) 100vw, 768px" /></noscript>															</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-70c6272 e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="70c6272" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-e2c6bb1 elementor-widget elementor-widget-text-editor" data-id="e2c6bb1" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="text-editor.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
									<p class="preFade fadeIn">In this section, we will walk you through placing a cryptocurrency transaction in the Bitcoin blockchain to show you what happens behind the scenes.</p><h3 class="preFade fadeIn">1. A Bitcoin Transaction Is Placed</h3><p class="preFade fadeIn">The first step in a bitcoin transaction is the placement of a trade from a <a href="https://www.tastytrade.com/concepts-strategies/types-of-crypto-wallets">crypto wallet</a>.  There are both custodial and <a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/blog/self-custody-wallet-explained">non-custodial crypto wallets</a>.</p><p class="preFade fadeIn">Whenever you place a crypto trade, you must attach a fee to it. As we will see later on, you must pay a miner (or a validator) a fee to validate your transaction so it can be added to a blockchain. Currently, this fee is about $1.44 for a Bitcoin transaction, regardless of your transaction size.</p><h3 class="preFade fadeIn">2. The Transaction Enters The Mempool</h3><p class="preFade fadeIn">Crypto transactions are not settled immediately; they must first be confirmed. The first step in the confirmation process is a ‘mempool’, which is essentially a waiting area for crypto transactions. </p><h3 class="preFade fadeIn">3. The Transaction Enters a ‘Block’</h3><p class="preFade fadeIn">As long as you attached a fee to your bitcoin transaction, a ‘miner’ (or ‘validator’ for Ethereum), will pull your transaction from the mempool and put it in a block. Miners then proceed to validate all transactions within this block by using algorithms to solve a complex mathematical problem. There will be only one winning miner per block. </p><p class="preFade fadeIn"><strong>Watch</strong>: <a href="https://www.tastytrade.com/shows/jones-and-grace/episodes/the-cost-of-mining-bitcoin-05-28-2021">The Cost Of Mining Bitcoin </a></p><h3 class="preFade fadeIn">4. The Order Is Verified</h3><p class="preFade fadeIn">After a block is validated (and the mathematical problem is solved), the block and its transactions are broadcasted to the entire Bitcoin network. The nodes within the network then make sure that none of the crypto within these transactions has already been spent (double-spending problem)..</p><h3 class="preFade fadeIn">5. Block Added To The Chain</h3><p class="preFade fadeIn">After all transactions are validated and verified, that block then gets added to the blockchain. </p><h3 class="preFade fadeIn">6. The Miner is Paid</h3><p class="preFade fadeIn">The winning miner then collects all of the fees that were attached to the transactions within the block as payment. A network may also pay additional rewards on top of these fees. Currently, the Bitcoin network rewards miners with 6.25 bitcoin (BTC) as payment for their services. </p><h3 class="preFade fadeIn">7. Transaction Settled</h3><p class="preFade fadeIn">The bitcoin transaction is now settled. The block is timestamped and linked to the previous block. However, a few additional blocks must be added to the blockchain before the transactions in a block are confirmed. Confirmation can take from a few minutes to an hour. </p>								</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-46d90a2 e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="46d90a2" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-a8e7045 elementor-widget elementor-widget-text-editor" data-id="a8e7045" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="text-editor.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
									<h2 class="preFade fadeIn">What Are the Different Types of Blockchains?</h2><p class="preFade fadeIn">There are four primary types of blockchains. The most common type is a ‘public’ blockchain. All popular blockchains, such as Bitcoin, Ethereum, Solana, and Litecoin, are public blockchains. <br /><br /></p><h3 class="preFade fadeIn">1. Public Blockchain</h3><p class="preFade fadeIn">Public blockchains are open to anyone. These permissionless decentralized networks use consensus mechanisms such as &#8220;proof-of-work&#8221; (<a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/blog/bitcoin">Bitcoin</a>) and &#8220;proof-of-stake&#8221; (<a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/blog/ethereum">Ethereum</a>) to reach an agreement on the current state of the network. This is also the process by which cryptocurrencies are created (more on this later). </p><h3 class="preFade fadeIn">2. Private Blockchain</h3><p class="preFade fadeIn">Private blockchains are controlled by a central authority. Permission is required in private blockchains to both place transactions and validate transactions. Because of the limited number of participants, private blockchains are vulnerable to bad actors. </p><h3 class="preFade fadeIn">3. Consortium Blockchains</h3><p class="preFade fadeIn">Consortium blockchains are like private blockchains in that they are centralized, but the control of these blockchains is diversified across numerous organizations. Private blockchains involve only one organization. Like private blockchains, parties must be invited to join consortium blockchains. Consortium blockchains are, therefore, both private and permissioned blockchains. </p><h3 class="preFade fadeIn">4. Hybrid Blockchains</h3><p class="preFade fadeIn">Hybrid blockchains combine elements of both public and private blockchains. They are like private blockchains in that they are controlled by a single entity and require permission. However, the validation of the blocks is performed by a public-facing interface. Hybrid blockchain solutions are a favorite of organizations such as IBM, who have launched the popular <a href="https://www.ibm.com/blockchain/solutions/food-trust">IBM&#8217;s Food Trust Blockchain</a>. </p><p class="preFade fadeIn">Additional use cases of hybrid blockchains (among others) can be seen in the healthcare sector. Here, blockchains provide an efficient and effective means of patient record-keeping and managing medicine supply chains. </p><h2 class="preFade fadeIn">What Is Cryptocurrency and How Does It Work?</h2><p class="preFade fadeIn">Cryptocurrencies are digital currencies that are stored and validated on a decentralized, peer-to-peer network. For this reason, cryptocurrencies exist without the need for an intermediary, such as a bank.</p><p class="preFade fadeIn">Cryptocurrencies use cryptography (encryption algorithms) to secure transactions within a network. The use of cryptography makes it difficult to double-spend crypto, a major problem that plagued digital assets before Bitcoin was invented.</p><h2 class="preFade fadeIn">How Are Cryptocurrencies Created?</h2>								</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-95d06a4 e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="95d06a4" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-5853bb8 elementor-widget elementor-widget-image" data-id="5853bb8" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="image.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
															<img decoding="async" width="800" height="419" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20800%20419'%3E%3C/svg%3E" class="attachment-large size-large wp-image-1616" alt="how cryptocurrencies are created" data-lazy-srcset="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-cryptocurrencies-are-created-1024x536.png 1024w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-cryptocurrencies-are-created-300x157.png 300w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-cryptocurrencies-are-created-768x402.png 768w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-cryptocurrencies-are-created.png 1200w" data-lazy-sizes="(max-width: 800px) 100vw, 800px" data-lazy-src="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-cryptocurrencies-are-created-1024x536.png" /><noscript><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="800" height="419" src="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-cryptocurrencies-are-created-1024x536.png" class="attachment-large size-large wp-image-1616" alt="how cryptocurrencies are created" srcset="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-cryptocurrencies-are-created-1024x536.png 1024w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-cryptocurrencies-are-created-300x157.png 300w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-cryptocurrencies-are-created-768x402.png 768w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/how-cryptocurrencies-are-created.png 1200w" sizes="(max-width: 800px) 100vw, 800px" /></noscript>															</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-a67a213 e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="a67a213" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-0fb10e4 elementor-widget elementor-widget-text-editor" data-id="0fb10e4" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="text-editor.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
									<p>The origin of all cryptocurrency can be traced back to a consensus mechanism. As we learned before, a consensus mechanism is simply the method by which a group of blockchain participants comes to an agreement on the state of a network.&nbsp;</p>
<p>There are two predominant consensus mechanisms:<strong> proof-of-work and proof-of-stake</strong>.&nbsp;</p>
<h3>Proof-of-Work Explained</h3>
<p>In proof-of-work blockchain networks, like Bitcoin, miners race to add a block to a blockchain by attempting to solve a cryptographic puzzle by employing algorithms. The miner that solves this complex math riddle first gets the honor of adding the next block to the blockchain.&nbsp;</p>
<p>For their work, a miner is rewarded by collecting the transaction fees in the block as well as an additional network reward.&nbsp; Currently, the block reward for Bitcoin is 6.25 bitcoin (BTC).</p>
<p>Massive computing power is necessary to compete in this space.</p>
<p>In proof-of-work networks, this consensus mechanism is the origin of all of a network&#8217;s coins. From this origin, the coins get bought and sold on both centralized and decentralized exchanges (DEXs).</p>
<h3>Proof-of-Stake Explained</h3>
<p>In proof-of-stake blockchains, like <a href="https://ethereum.org/en/">Ethereum</a>, a network randomly selects a node (computer) to do the math required to validate transactions and add the block that those transactions reside in onto the blockchain.&nbsp;</p>
<p>In proof-of-stake, these participants are called validators. Validators ‘stake’ crypto coins to a network. The network selects a staked coin at random and does the math required on this stakers computer to secure and advance the network. The more coins a validator stakes, the greater the odds that validator has of being chose to validate.&nbsp;</p>
<p>Proof-of-stake coins are created through this consensus mechanism as well. However, after enough coins are given out to validators, proof-of-stake networks can choose to stop minting new coins and simply pay validators in transaction fees. This helps to control a networks supply of coins (monetary policy).&nbsp;</p>
<h2>Bitcoin vs Ethereum: What’s The Difference?</h2>
<p>We mentioned before that the Bitcoin network uses a proof-of-work consensus mechanism while Ethereum uses a proof-of-stake consensus mechanism.&nbsp;</p>
<p>Aside from this differentiator, these two networks diverge in what their blockchains can actually store.&nbsp;</p>
<p><strong>The Bitcoin</strong> <strong>network</strong> can store only simple transactions, such as the buying and selling of BTC, in its blocks.&nbsp;</p>
<p><strong>The Ethereum network</strong> can store both transactions <em>and </em>run code. This code comes in the form of programs called smart contracts.&nbsp;</p>
<p><span style="text-align: var(--text-align);"><b>⚠️&nbsp;</b></span><em>Ethereum recently switched from a proof-of-work to a proof-of-stake network. </em><a href="https://www.tastytrade.com/news-insights/ethereum-merge-explained"><em>Read about the ‘Merge’ here</em></a><em>.&nbsp;</em></p>
<h2>What Are Smart Contracts?</h2>								</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-11075bf e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="11075bf" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-6f8b8dd elementor-widget elementor-widget-image" data-id="6f8b8dd" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="image.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
															<img decoding="async" width="800" height="419" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20800%20419'%3E%3C/svg%3E" class="attachment-large size-large wp-image-1617" alt="smart contracts" data-lazy-srcset="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/smart-contracts-1024x536.png 1024w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/smart-contracts-300x157.png 300w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/smart-contracts-768x402.png 768w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/smart-contracts.png 1200w" data-lazy-sizes="(max-width: 800px) 100vw, 800px" data-lazy-src="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/smart-contracts-1024x536.png" /><noscript><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="800" height="419" src="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/smart-contracts-1024x536.png" class="attachment-large size-large wp-image-1617" alt="smart contracts" srcset="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/smart-contracts-1024x536.png 1024w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/smart-contracts-300x157.png 300w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/smart-contracts-768x402.png 768w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/smart-contracts.png 1200w" sizes="(max-width: 800px) 100vw, 800px" /></noscript>															</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-490f202 e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="490f202" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-d43dbde elementor-widget elementor-widget-text-editor" data-id="d43dbde" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="text-editor.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
									<p><a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/blog/smart-contracts">Smart contracts</a> are used to execute agreements between parties automatically, with no intermediary required. Smart contracts can be thought of as vending machines in terms of simple inputs and output: when you put a dollar in a vending machine, you get a bag of chips. Smart contracts use the same logic. No third party is required. Like all things in blockchain, smart contracts are immutable. </p><p>Smart contracts are the lifeblood of Web3. In this latest interation of the internet, versions of popular social media apps, and even entire financial institutions, are becoming rebuilt on <a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/blog/dapps">decentralized blockchain applications</a>. DeFi (decentralized finance) is the term used to describe blockchain&#8217;s growing presence in traditionally centralized financial services.  </p><p><a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/blog/what-is-defi">📕 Read! What is DeFi And How Does it Work?</a></p><h2>What Are NFTs?</h2>								</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-d95bc3d e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="d95bc3d" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-60aa7f6 elementor-widget elementor-widget-image" data-id="60aa7f6" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="image.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
															<img decoding="async" width="768" height="402" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20768%20402'%3E%3C/svg%3E" class="attachment-medium_large size-medium_large wp-image-1618" alt="nft information" data-lazy-srcset="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/nft-information-768x402.png 768w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/nft-information-300x157.png 300w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/nft-information-1024x536.png 1024w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/nft-information.png 1200w" data-lazy-sizes="(max-width: 768px) 100vw, 768px" data-lazy-src="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/nft-information-768x402.png" /><noscript><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="768" height="402" src="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/nft-information-768x402.png" class="attachment-medium_large size-medium_large wp-image-1618" alt="nft information" srcset="https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/nft-information-768x402.png 768w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/nft-information-300x157.png 300w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/nft-information-1024x536.png 1024w, https://www.tastycrypto.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/nft-information.png 1200w" sizes="(max-width: 768px) 100vw, 768px" /></noscript>															</div>
				</div>
					</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-ef848ed e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent" data-id="ef848ed" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-a277251 elementor-widget elementor-widget-text-editor" data-id="a277251" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="text-editor.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
									<p>Most cryptocurrency is fungible. Fungible simply means interchangeable. For example, US dollars are fungible because any one US dollar is as good as the next. Bitcoins too are fungible: every bitcoin has the same exact value. </p><p>The acronym “NFT” stands for <a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/blog/nfts">non-fungible token</a>. NFTs are <em>not</em> interchangeable. The paintings of Monet, for example, are non-fungible because they all have different values. </p><p> In crypto, NFTs are simply non-fungible digital assets. NFTs can come in the form of digital art or even sports clips. When a digital asset is unique and can not be interchanged, it is considered an NFT. </p><p><strong>Next</strong>: <a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/blog/cryptocurrency-for-beginners"><strong>What is Cryptocurrency and How Does it Work?</strong></a></p>								</div>
				</div>
		<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-7a97d50 e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-child" data-id="7a97d50" data-element_type="container" data-e-type="container">
					<div class="e-con-inner">
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-47f37b7 elementor-widget elementor-widget-heading" data-id="47f37b7" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="heading.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
					<h2 class="elementor-heading-title elementor-size-default">FAQs</h2>				</div>
				</div>
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-a076633 elementor-widget elementor-widget-accordion" data-id="a076633" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-settings="{&quot;ekit_we_effect_on&quot;:&quot;none&quot;}" data-widget_type="accordion.default">
				<div class="elementor-widget-container">
							<div class="elementor-accordion">
							<div class="elementor-accordion-item">
					<div id="elementor-tab-title-1681" class="elementor-tab-title" data-tab="1" role="button" aria-controls="elementor-tab-content-1681" aria-expanded="false">
													<span class="elementor-accordion-icon elementor-accordion-icon-left" aria-hidden="true">
															<span class="elementor-accordion-icon-closed"><svg class="e-font-icon-svg e-fas-plus" viewBox="0 0 448 512" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M416 208H272V64c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32h-32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v144H32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v32c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h144v144c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h32c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32V304h144c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32v-32c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32z"></path></svg></span>
								<span class="elementor-accordion-icon-opened"><svg class="e-font-icon-svg e-fas-minus" viewBox="0 0 448 512" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M416 208H32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v32c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h384c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32v-32c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32z"></path></svg></span>
														</span>
												<a class="elementor-accordion-title" tabindex="0">What is a Crypto Wallet?</a>
					</div>
					<div id="elementor-tab-content-1681" class="elementor-tab-content elementor-clearfix" data-tab="1" role="region" aria-labelledby="elementor-tab-title-1681"><p>In order to transact on a blockchain, you will need a private key. Private keys are often represented by seed phrases. </p><p>Your private key is like your bank password in that anyone that has access to it can access your crypto. Crypto wallets hold your private key for you. <a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/blog/web3-wallets">Web3 wallets</a> can be either software or hardware. If a centralized exchange, such as Coinbase, holds your crypto on your behalf, they will store your private key for you. This is called a custodial wallet. </p><p><strong><em>📚 Read! </em></strong><a href="https://www.tastycrypto.com/blog/private-key-vs-seed-phrase-vs-recovery"><strong><em>Private Keys vs Seed Phrases vs Recovery Phrases</em></strong></a></p></div>
				</div>
							<div class="elementor-accordion-item">
					<div id="elementor-tab-title-1682" class="elementor-tab-title" data-tab="2" role="button" aria-controls="elementor-tab-content-1682" aria-expanded="false">
													<span class="elementor-accordion-icon elementor-accordion-icon-left" aria-hidden="true">
															<span class="elementor-accordion-icon-closed"><svg class="e-font-icon-svg e-fas-plus" viewBox="0 0 448 512" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M416 208H272V64c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32h-32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v144H32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v32c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h144v144c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h32c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32V304h144c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32v-32c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32z"></path></svg></span>
								<span class="elementor-accordion-icon-opened"><svg class="e-font-icon-svg e-fas-minus" viewBox="0 0 448 512" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M416 208H32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v32c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h384c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32v-32c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32z"></path></svg></span>
														</span>
												<a class="elementor-accordion-title" tabindex="0">What Is the Main Purpose of Blockchain?</a>
					</div>
					<div id="elementor-tab-content-1682" class="elementor-tab-content elementor-clearfix" data-tab="2" role="region" aria-labelledby="elementor-tab-title-1682"><p>The main purpose of blockchain technology is to provide a safe and transparent record of distributed transactions without the need for an intermediary. </p></div>
				</div>
							<div class="elementor-accordion-item">
					<div id="elementor-tab-title-1683" class="elementor-tab-title" data-tab="3" role="button" aria-controls="elementor-tab-content-1683" aria-expanded="false">
													<span class="elementor-accordion-icon elementor-accordion-icon-left" aria-hidden="true">
															<span class="elementor-accordion-icon-closed"><svg class="e-font-icon-svg e-fas-plus" viewBox="0 0 448 512" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M416 208H272V64c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32h-32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v144H32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v32c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h144v144c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h32c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32V304h144c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32v-32c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32z"></path></svg></span>
								<span class="elementor-accordion-icon-opened"><svg class="e-font-icon-svg e-fas-minus" viewBox="0 0 448 512" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M416 208H32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v32c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h384c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32v-32c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32z"></path></svg></span>
														</span>
												<a class="elementor-accordion-title" tabindex="0">What Are The Benefits of Blockchain?</a>
					</div>
					<div id="elementor-tab-content-1683" class="elementor-tab-content elementor-clearfix" data-tab="3" role="region" aria-labelledby="elementor-tab-title-1683"><p>Blockchain has many benefits over traditionally centralized ledgers. Some of these benefits include speed, efficiency, pseudonymity, immutability, traceability, and transparency. </p></div>
				</div>
							<div class="elementor-accordion-item">
					<div id="elementor-tab-title-1684" class="elementor-tab-title" data-tab="4" role="button" aria-controls="elementor-tab-content-1684" aria-expanded="false">
													<span class="elementor-accordion-icon elementor-accordion-icon-left" aria-hidden="true">
															<span class="elementor-accordion-icon-closed"><svg class="e-font-icon-svg e-fas-plus" viewBox="0 0 448 512" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M416 208H272V64c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32h-32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v144H32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v32c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h144v144c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h32c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32V304h144c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32v-32c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32z"></path></svg></span>
								<span class="elementor-accordion-icon-opened"><svg class="e-font-icon-svg e-fas-minus" viewBox="0 0 448 512" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M416 208H32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v32c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h384c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32v-32c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32z"></path></svg></span>
														</span>
												<a class="elementor-accordion-title" tabindex="0">What Are The Top 5 Blockchains?</a>
					</div>
					<div id="elementor-tab-content-1684" class="elementor-tab-content elementor-clearfix" data-tab="4" role="region" aria-labelledby="elementor-tab-title-1684"><p>The top five blockchains by market cap are, respectively: Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH), Binance (BNB), Cardano (ADA), and Dogecoin (DOGE).</p><p>📕 <strong>Read</strong>: <a href="https://www.tastytrade.com/concepts-strategies/btc-vs-eth-vs-sol-vs-matic">Bitcoin vs Ethereum vs Solana vs Polygon: Which Is Best?</a></p></div>
				</div>
							<div class="elementor-accordion-item">
					<div id="elementor-tab-title-1685" class="elementor-tab-title" data-tab="5" role="button" aria-controls="elementor-tab-content-1685" aria-expanded="false">
													<span class="elementor-accordion-icon elementor-accordion-icon-left" aria-hidden="true">
															<span class="elementor-accordion-icon-closed"><svg class="e-font-icon-svg e-fas-plus" viewBox="0 0 448 512" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M416 208H272V64c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32h-32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v144H32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v32c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h144v144c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h32c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32V304h144c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32v-32c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32z"></path></svg></span>
								<span class="elementor-accordion-icon-opened"><svg class="e-font-icon-svg e-fas-minus" viewBox="0 0 448 512" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M416 208H32c-17.67 0-32 14.33-32 32v32c0 17.67 14.33 32 32 32h384c17.67 0 32-14.33 32-32v-32c0-17.67-14.33-32-32-32z"></path></svg></span>
														</span>
												<a class="elementor-accordion-title" tabindex="0">What Are The Main Disadvantages of Blockchain?</a>
					</div>
					<div id="elementor-tab-content-1685" class="elementor-tab-content elementor-clearfix" data-tab="5" role="region" aria-labelledby="elementor-tab-title-1685"><div class="elementor-element elementor-element-a277251 elementor-widget elementor-widget-text-editor" data-id="a277251" data-element_type="widget" data-widget_type="text-editor.default"><div class="elementor-widget-container"><p>A main disadvantage of blockchain today is that centralized exchanges control over 90% of all crypto transactions. This centralization of power exposes blockchains to centralized risks, which was seen recently with the fall of FTX. </p><p>Additionally, there are very few regulations in place pertaining to cryptocurrency, which hinders crypto adoption.  </p></div></div></div>
				</div>

Crypto wallets hold your private key for you.\u00a0<a href=\"https:\/\/www.tastycrypto.com\/blog\/web3-wallets\">Web3 wallets<\/a>\u00a0can be either software or hardware. {"prefetch":[{"source":"document","where":{"and":[{"href_matches":"/*"},{"not":{"href_matches":["/wp-*.php","/wp-admin/*","/wp-content/uploads/*","/wp-content/*","/wp-content/plugins/*","/wp-content/themes/hello-elementor/*","/*\\?(.+)"]}},{"not":{"selector_matches":"a[rel~=\"nofollow\"]"}},{"not":{"selector_matches":".no-prefetch, .no-prefetch a"}}]},"eagerness":"conservative"}]}

			<script>
				( () => {
					const lazyloadRunObserver = () => {
						const lazyloadBackgrounds = document.querySelectorAll( `.e-con.e-parent:not(.e-lazyloaded)` );
						const lazyloadBackgroundObserver = new IntersectionObserver( ( entries ) => {
							entries.forEach( ( entry ) => {
								if ( entry.isIntersecting ) {
									let lazyloadBackground = entry.target;
									if( lazyloadBackground ) {
										lazyloadBackground.classList.add( 'e-lazyloaded' );
									}
									lazyloadBackgroundObserver.unobserve( entry.target );
								}
							});
						}, { rootMargin: '200px 0px 200px 0px' } );
						lazyloadBackgrounds.forEach( ( lazyloadBackground ) => {
							lazyloadBackgroundObserver.observe( lazyloadBackground );
						} );
					};
					const events = [
						'DOMContentLoaded',
						'elementor/lazyload/observe',
					];
					events.forEach( ( event ) => {
						document.addEventListener( event, lazyloadRunObserver );
					} );
				} )();
			</script>
